r1

=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
In the nation of Atcoder, there are N cities numbered 1 to N, and M trains numbered 1 to M.
Train i departs from city A_i at time S_i and arrives at city B_i at time T_i.
Given a positive integer X_1, find a way to set non-negative integers X_2,\ldots,X_M that satisfies the following condition with the minimum possible value of X_2+\ldots+X_M.

- Condition: For all pairs (i,j) satisfying 1 \leq i,j \leq M, if B_i=A_j and T_i \leq S_j, then T_i+X_i \leq S_j+X_j.
- In other words, for any pair of trains that are originally possible to transfer between, it is still possible to transfer even after delaying the departure and arrival times of each train i by X_i.



It can be proved that such a way to set X_2,\ldots,X_M with the minimum possible value of X_2+\ldots+X_M is unique.

Input

The input is given from Standard Input in the following format:
N M X_1
A_1 B_1 S_1 T_1
\vdots
A_M B_M S_M T_M

Output

Print X_2,\ldots,X_M that satisfy the condition with the minimum possible sum, in that order, separated by spaces.

Constraints


- 2 \leq N \leq 2\times 10^5
- 2 \leq M \leq 2\times 10^5
- 1 \leq A_i,B_i \leq N
- A_i \neq B_i
- 0 \leq S_i < T_i \leq 10^9
- 1 \leq X_1 \leq 10^9
- All input values are integers.

Sample Input 1

3 6 15
1 2 10 20
1 2 20 30
2 3 25 40
2 3 35 50
3 1 15 30
3 1 45 60

Sample Output 1

0 10 0 0 5

The arrival of train 1 from city 1 to 2 is delayed by 15 and becomes time 35.
To allow transfer from train 1 to 3 in city 2, the departure of train 3 is delayed by 10, making it depart at time 35 and arrive at time 50.
Further, to allow transfer from train 3 to 6 in city 3, the departure of train 6 is delayed by 5, making it depart at time 50.
Other trains can operate without delay while still allowing transfers between originally transferable trains, so (X_2,X_3,X_4,X_5,X_6)=(0,10,0,0,5) satisfies the condition.
Moreover, there is no solution with a smaller sum that satisfies the condition, so this is the answer.

Sample Input 2

10 9 100
1 10 0 1
10 2 1 100
10 3 1 100
10 4 1 100
10 5 1 100
10 6 1 100
10 7 1 100
10 8 1 100
10 9 1 100

Sample Output 2

100 100 100 100 100 100 100 100

Sample Input 3

4 4 10
1 2 0 1
1 2 0 10
2 3 100 200
2 4 100 200

Sample Output 3

0 0 0

Reason in a commit-implement-recheck way.

A productive rhythm for this task usually looks like this:
- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Output only the final Python code enclosed in a code block.

We need for each city c:

We can coordinate-compress per city all arrival times T_i for trains with B_i=c. Let's call city_events[c] = sorted unique T_i of trains arriving at city c. Then for each city, a segment tree/Fenwick min supporting point update min and prefix min.

Fenwick for prefix min with point update min: Fenwick usually supports prefix min with update decreasing values if we update at index p and query prefix [1..p]. But if point update can only decrease, yes we insert min; values may be negative. Fenwick initialized INF. update(pos, val): while pos <= size: tree[pos] = min(tree[pos], val); pos += pos&-pos. query(pos): res=INF; while pos>0: res=min(res, tree[pos]); pos -= pos&-pos; return res. This works for prefix min with point updates (only decreases). Since each train inserted once with D_i-T_i; if D_i inf, skip. If multiple updates same pos, min. Good. Need map T to compressed index. For query prefix T <= S_j, find rightmost index with arrival_time <= S_j via bisect_right on city_arrival_times[c], then Fenwick query(idx). If idx=0, no arrivals.

Fenwick functions:
def update(bit, idx, val):
    n = len(bit)-1
    while idx <= n:
        if val < bit[idx]: bit[idx]=val
        idx += idx & -idx

def query(bit, idx):
    res = INF
    while idx >0:
        if bit[idx] < res: res=bit[idx]
        idx -= idx & -idx
    return res

Could precompute pos_arrival[i] for each train to avoid repeated? We insert each train once; bisect okay. But query also bisect_right. Total 2M bisections, okay.

Pseudo:

import sys, bisect
def main():
    input = sys.stdin.buffer.readline
    N,M,X1 = map(int,input().split())
    A=[0]*M; B=[0]*M; S=[0]*M; T=[0]*M
    arr_cities=[[] for _ in range(N+1)]
    for i in range(M):
        a,b,s,t=map(int,input().split())
        A[i]=a; B[i]=b; S[i]=s; T[i]=t
        arr_cities[b].append(t)
    times=[None]*(N+1)
    bit=[None]*(N+1)
    for c in range(1,N+1):
        if arr_cities[c]:
            lst=sorted(set(arr_cities[c]))
            times[c]=lst
            bit[c]=[INF]*(len(lst)+1)
    INF=10**30
    D=[INF]*M
    D[0]=0
    order=sorted(range(M), key=S.__getitem__)  # key=lambda i: S[i]
    for idx in order:
        if idx == 0:
            D[0]=0
        else:
            c = A[idx]
            if times[c] is not None:
                pos = bisect.bisect_right(times[c], S[idx])
                if pos > 0:
                    mn = query(bit[c], pos)
                    if mn < INF:
                        D[idx] = S[idx] + mn
            # else no arrivals
        # insert this train as arrival at B[idx] if D[idx] < INF
        if D[idx] < INF:
            c = B[idx]
            # times[c] should not be None because arr_cities[c] has T[idx]
            pos = bisect.bisect_left(times[c], T[idx]) + 1
            val = D[idx] - T[idx]
            update(bit[c], pos, val)
    ans = []
    for i in range(1,M):
        if D[i] >= INF:
            ans.append('0')
        else:
            v = X1 - D[i]
            if v < 0: v = 0
            ans.append(str(v))
    print(' '.join(ans))

Let's write helper functions inside main for speed:
from bisect import bisect_left, bisect_right
INF = 10**30

def bit_update(bit, idx, val):
    n = len(bit) - 1
    while idx <= n:
        if val < bit[idx]:
            bit[idx] = val
        idx += idx & -idx

def bit_query(bit, idx):
    res = INF
    while idx > 0:
        v = bit[idx]
        if v < res:
            res = v
        idx -= idx & -idx
    return res

Function call overhead 2e5*log? Actually update called for each reachable train, query for each train. 4e5 calls, each with loop ~18, function overhead okay. But note bit_update called only if D finite. In worst case all reachable, 2e5 updates. Query for all 2e5. Good.

Pseudo with helper functions:

def main():
    import sys
    from bisect import bisect_left, bisect_right
    input = sys.stdin.buffer.readline
    N, M, X1 = map(int, input().split())
    A=[0]*M; B=[0]*M; S=[0]*M; T=[0]*M
    arr_cities = {}
    for i in range(M):
        a,b,s,t = map(int, input().split())
        A[i]=a; B[i]=b; S[i]=s; T[i]=t
        arr_cities.setdefault(b, []).append(t)
    INF = 10**30
    times = [None]*(N+1)
    bit = [None]*(N+1)
    for c, lst in arr_cities.items():
        uniq = sorted(set(lst))
        times[c] = uniq
        bit[c] = [INF]*(len(uniq)+1)

    def update(c, t, val):
        # c city, t arrival time, val D-T
        pos = bisect_left(times[c], t) + 1
        btree = bit[c]
        n = len(btree) - 1
        while pos <= n:
            if val < btree[pos]:
                btree[pos] = val
            pos += pos & -pos

    def query(c, s):
        tc = times[c]
        if tc is None:
            return INF
        pos = bisect_right(tc, s)
        if pos == 0:
            return INF
        btree = bit[c]
        res = INF
        while pos > 0:
            v = btree[pos]
            if v < res:
                res = v
            pos -= pos & -pos
        return res

    D = [INF]*M
    D[0] = 0
    order = sorted(range(M), key=S.__getitem__)
    for idx in order:
        if idx == 0:
            D[idx] = 0
        else:
            mn = query(A[idx], S[idx])
            if mn < INF:
                D[idx] = S[idx] + mn
        if D[idx] < INF:
            update(B[idx], T[idx], D[idx] - T[idx])
    ans = []
    for i in range(1, M):
        if D[i] >= INF:
            ans.append("0")
        else:
            v = X1 - D[i]
            if v < 0:
                v = 0
            ans.append(str(v))
    sys.stdout.write(" ".join(ans))

Need ensure D[0]=0 before loop. If order sorted, when idx==0, D already 0. If idx !=0, query may return INF. Good.

But wait: If source is not first, and we process earlier trains before source, D[0] is 0 but source not inserted. Could an earlier train j with S_j < S0 be reachable from source via a path where the first edge is from source to some train k with S_k > S0, then ... to j? No, S strictly increases, so cannot. Good.

Now, let's think about if source is not first, and an earlier train j has A_j = B0 and S_j >= T0? impossible because S_j < S0 < T0. So even if source inserted, not included. So Approach B and A equivalent.
